We can use iterative algorithms for peak autocorrelation:

Given a (nonnegative). We want minimize R = max b / S^2 *2n. Fix S=1. Define b = fft convolution.
Gradient of objective with respect to a (when max at lag k, assuming unique) for unconstrained (allow negative) is ∇b_k = a reversed/padded: for linear convolution, b_k = sum_i a_i a_{k-i}. If we compute b = conv(a,a), gradient wrt a is 2 * cross-correlation? But for nonnegative and scale. We can do projected gradient: update a = a - η * grad where grad is the "active lag correlate": if max at k, reduce a_i where a_{k-i} large, i.e. subtract η * a[k-i] (plus possible a[k-i-n+1]? Need exact gradient of b_k wrt a_j:
b_k = sum_i a_i a_{k-i}, with valid 0≤i<n,0≤k-i<n.
Term involving a_j: when i=j: a_{k-j}; when k-i=j => i=k-j: a_{k-j} (if j != k-j) gives another a_j * a_j? Actually derivative:
∂b_k/∂a_j = number? b_k = ∑_i a_i a_{k-i}. Treat a_i independent. Derivative = a_{k-j} (from i=j) + a_{j}?? Let's do product rule: d/d a_j ∑ a_i a_{k-i}. Terms where i=j: a_{k-j}. Terms where k-i=j => i=k-j: a_j? derivative of a_{k-i} with respect to a_j is 1 if k-i=j, yielding a_i with i=k-j => a_{k-j}. So if j != k-j, derivative = 2 a_{k-j}. If 2j=k, term i=j=k-j, derivative of a_j^2 is 2a_j. So generally ∂b_k/∂a_j = 2 a_{k-j} if k-j valid, else 0? Check if k-j valid and j valid. What if k-j outside but j valid? No term. If j=k-j, 2a_j. So gradient is 2 * padded a shifted at index k-j. For b_k, gradient vector = 2 * reverse? Specifically grad[j]=2*a[k-j] if 0≤k-j<n else 0. This is 2 times a reversed segment. For full convolution all lags, gradient of sum active = 2 * sum_{active k} a[k-j] (with valid).

Thus for maximizing? We minimize max b, so at active lag k*, gradient points to increase b_k. To reduce, we should move a in negative gradient direction: a_j = a_j - η * a[k*-j] (times 2 maybe). But this can make negative; project to nonnegative and renormalize. This is like deconvolution/water-filling. It may work.

But direct projected gradient on max may be slow and can get stuck. We can use smooth approx or active sets.

Alternative: use "iterative reweighted" to shape a such that b is flat. For given desired peak M, constraints b_k ≤ M. We can find a by minimizing ∑(a_i - a_i_old)^2 subject to b≤M? Nonconvex. But we can use bisection and projected gradient to satisfy constraints.

Another route: This is a quadratic programming with a max variable. Could formulate as:
minimize t
subject to ∑_i a_i a_{k-i} ≤ t for all k
∑a_i=1, a_i≥0.
This is nonconvex because constraints are indefinite quadratic? However, b_k is bilinear in same a, but for fixed k, the quadratic form matrix has rank 2? Could be nonconvex. But maybe if we symmetrize? Not convex.

Let's design numerical optimizer:
- Choose n (e.g., 256, 512, 1024) small enough for many calls.
- Parameterize a as positive part of a sine-wave or use variables log a to ensure positivity and normalization.
- Objective: smooth max of b: soft_max(b) = (1/β) logsumexp(β b). Since b up to? scale fixed sum=1, b ~1/n. Use β large.
- Constraints: sum a=1, a_i>0. Use scipy.optimize.minimize on log variables y, a=softmax(y)/n? scale. This is smooth, nonconvex but can run.
- Need good initial y. Maybe from a Gaussian or ramp or random.

But we need final program robust in 1500s, one CPU. Could run several n and multiple random inits, keep best. Scipy optimize might be slow for n=1024 but maybe okay if limited. We can compute convolution via np.convolve O(n^2) each eval; for L-BFGS on 1024 variables, each iteration many evals too slow. Need gradient and faster convolution via FFT? But evaluate_sequence itself uses np.convolve O(n^2). Our internal estimates can use FFT for speed, but final score validated. We can use FFT to compute convolution for scoring once (or numpy.fft). But the given evaluate_sequence uses np.convolve; for n=1000 O1e6 ok. For n=5000 O25e6 per call heavy. Use n maybe 512-2048 and FFT internally for optimization. At end, call evaluate_sequence on candidates.

Can use `np.fft.fft` to compute linear convolution quickly O(n log n). For nonnegative a, conv = real(ifft(fft(a,nfft)**2)).

Optimization via projected gradient on b directly could be faster: We can implement our own active-set subgradient descent without scipy.

Subgradient descent for min max b: maintain a normalized nonnegative. At each step, compute b. Identify active set (top p lags). Gradient g_j = 2∑_{k in active weights} a[k-j] (with valid). Update a_j -= η g_j. Then project to nonnegative and renormalize. This is O(n + active n) per step. We can adapt η. This is like heat equation with source depending on active lags. It may flatten b.

But uniform: active center gradient constant, no change. Need break symmetry. Initialize with non-symmetric perturbations. The subgradient might move to lower basin. We can also use stochastic active lag (sample one max) to avoid constant.

Another method: "iterative peel" based on constraints b_k ≤ M. Choose target M. Use projected subgradient to satisfy constraints (like CVX). Or use multiplicative updates: a_j ← a_j * exp(-η ∑ active a[k-j])? Multiplicative keeps nonnegative. Similar to gradient in log space. We can use log variables: y_j updated y_j ← y_j - η g_j, a=exp(y-Max?) normalize. This may handle positivity.

Could also use Nesterov accelerated gradient for smooth logsumexp approximation. We can implement L-BFGS? Simpler: Use `scipy.optimize.minimize` with BFGS and analytic gradient on moderate n (e.g., 128) with logsumexp objective. But may get local minima.

Let's implement internal scoring fast:
```
def score_from_array(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    S=a.sum()
    if S<0.01: return inf
    # use FFT convolution for speed; nfft=1<<(2*n-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,n=nfft)
    b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,n=nfft)[:2*n-1]
    # due to numerical small neg, max real
    maxb=b.max()
    return 2*n*maxb/(S*S)
```
For n up to 4096, FFT quick. Accuracy okay. For final, call evaluate_sequence.

We need ensure sequence values not boolean etc; list floats.

Optimization methods in detail:

Base generator:
```
def base_seq(n):
    a=np.empty(n)
    val=1.0
    a[0]=1
    for i in range(1,n):
        # c_i = c_{i-1} * (2i-1)/(2i) ? central binomial/4^i: c_i = c_{i-1} * (2i-1)/(2i)
        val *= (2*i -1)/(2*i)
        a[i]=val
    return a
```
Check i=1: val*1/2=0.5; i=2: *3/4=0.375; yes.

Score of base unnormalized max? For n large, unnormalized first n b=1; tail maybe <=1? Need verify tail not >1. For base prefix, is full convolution max always 1? We assumed tail below. Let's prove? For k≥n, b_k = ∑_{i=k-n+1}^{n-1} c_i c_{k-i}. The infinite coefficient is 1 = b_k + missing terms (positive), so b_k ≤1. Yes. Thus max=1. Good.

Parametric variants:
1. Exponential tilt: a_i = c_i * r^i, 0<r< maybe 2. For r>1, tail increases and max may occur later; but unnormalized max not 1. Score scale invariant. We can scan log r from -3 to 1.
2. Low-order polynomial tilt: a_i = c_i * (1 + u * i/(n-1) + v * (i/(n-1))^2) ensuring positive.
3. Reflected addition: a_i = c_i + α c_rev[i] (c_rev[i]=c_{n-1-i}). α in [0,1]. This symmetrizes slightly.
4. Shifted addition: a_i = c_i + α c_{i-m} for i≥m, with c_{negative}=0, m in [n//4, 3n//4], α small [0,0.5]. But earlier may not help; still scan.
5. Suffix boost: a_i *= 1 + β * smoothstep((i - floor(0.4n))/0.2n) maybe; and prefix boost.
6. Power law: a_i = (i+1)^p for p? Base p=-0.5? Actually c_i ~1/√(i+1). Use p from -1 to 0.3.

But we don't know which helps; local search can after param scan.

Local stochastic search:
- Use array unnormalized. Objective score. We can use hill climbing with adaptive step. Since score landscape maybe flat. Use block multiplicative perturbations:
```
def perturb(a,rng,block_power=0.1):
   n=a.size
   width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//200), max(2,n//8)))
   start=rng.integers(0,n-width)
   factor=np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma, size=width))
   b=a.copy(); b[start:start+width]*=factor
   return np.clip(b,0,1000)
```
Previous sigma=0.12. We can use smaller 0.05, 0.1, and also additive small. For unnormalized base values vary from1 to~0.02, relative perturbations okay.
- Accept if score < best_score*(1-1e-9). Keep best.
- Use random restarts: start from base with random perturbations of magnitude 0.05-0.3.
- To avoid stuck at base, use "simulated annealing": accept worse with probability exp(-(score-best)/T) for some T? But we need best; can occasionally.

def mirror_descent(a, steps, betas):
   z=np.log(a+1e-300) # normalized? keep sum maybe
   for beta in betas:
      for it in range(steps):
         compute b fft
         # softprobs over b: use b scaled
         # b values ~1/n. beta maybe 10*n? Need stable: beta = 20*n? then βb ~20, ok.
         br = b - b.max()
         w=np.exp(beta*br)
         w/=w.sum()
         # compute G = cross-correlation of w with a: G[j]=∑_k w[k] a[k-j] (factor ignored)
         # Use FFT: reverse a? For each k, shift a by k? We can compute G via convolution: G = ifft(fft(w) * fft(padded reversed a))

def propose_candidate(seed=0,budget_s=1500):
   best_seq=None; best_score=inf
   lengths = [8,16,32,64,128,256,512,1024,2048]
   # allocate time adaptively
   for n in lengths:
       a=base_seq(n)
       # param scans
       candidates = parametric_candidates(a,n)
       for cand in candidates:
           s=score_fast(cand)
           if s<best_score: update
       # improvement: mirror_descent and stochastic on best few
       a_best = best for this n
       run_leveled_mirror(a_best, time_budget)
       run_stochastic(a_best, time_budget)
       validate final by evaluate_sequence for top candidates (not too many)
   return best_seq.tolist()
```

```
def level_sequence(a, iters, eta, alpha, topk):
    z = log(a)
    S_best = a.sum()
    for it in range(iters):
        av = exp(z - max(z)) # arbitrary scale
        b = conv(av)
        maxb = b.max()
        if maxb<=0: break
        # rescale to max=1
        scale = 1/sqrt(maxb)
        av *= scale
        S = av.sum()
        if S > S_best: S_best=S_best; z=log(av)
        # target
        target = maxb * (1-alpha)
        # choose top weights: lambda = max(0,b-target)/maxb
        # We can use all b? compute G via top indices.
        idx = np.argsort(b)[-topk:] if topk < len(b) else range(len(b))
        # But early lags all equal; argsort arbitrary. To include early lags more representatively, maybe idx = random sample among b>target plus top.
        G = zeros(n)
        for k in idx:
            lam = (b[k]-target)/maxb
            if lam<=0: continue
            lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); hi=min(n-1,k)
            # segment a[lo:hi+1] corresponds to a[k-j]? If j from lo to hi, k-j ranges from k-lo down to k-hi. We need G[j]+=lam*a[k-j]. We can add lam * av[lo:hi+1][::-1]? Let's map: For j in [lo,hi], index t=k-j. When j=lo, t=k-lo; when j=hi, t=k-hi. So t decreases as j increases. The array av[t] for t from k-lo down to k-hi. We need G[lo:hi+1] += lam * av[k-lo : k-hi-1 : -1]. Check.
            G[lo:hi+1] += lam * av[lo2:hi2+1][::-1] where lo2=k-hi, hi2=k-lo.
```

G[lo:hi+1] += lam * av[t_lo:t_hi+1][::-1]
```

Score_fast:
```
def score_fast(a):
   a = np.asarray(a,float).copy()
   a[np.isnan|~finite]=0; a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   n=a.size; S=a.sum()
   if S<1e-12: return inf
   # if n>8192? not use
   N=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
   A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
   b=np.fft.irfft(A*A,N)[:2*n-1]
   maxb=float(b.max())
   if maxb<=0: return inf
   return float(2*n*maxb/(S*S))
```
Values can be rescaled to avoid sum huge; for internal, we can normalize sum=1 before score to avoid overflow. But score invariant. In level, we keep max=1, sum maybe √n; fine.

For candidate final, values not need normalized; but ensure sum>0.01. Since score invariant, we can scale to sum=1 maybe values ≤0.1? For n=2048 base values max ~1, sum~? If unnormalized base sum~72, okay; if scale to sum1, max ~0.014, fine.

Level_step implementation with dense λ:
```
def level_step(z, alpha, eta):
    a=exp(z - max(z))
    N=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    b=np.fft.irfft(A*A,N)[:2*n-1]
    maxb=b.max()
    if maxb<=0: return None
    a *= 1/np.sqrt(maxb)
    # recompute A? since scaled, A_scaled=A/sqrt(maxb)
    A_scaled=A/np.sqrt(maxb)
    S=a.sum()
    # current score after scale=2*n/(S*S)
    target=maxb*(1-alpha)
    lam=np.maximum(0, b-target) # b unscaled? b scales? target based maxb unscaled. lam scale same as b. For G, use a_scaled. Use lam_unscaled; okay scale.
    # maybe normalize lam by sum to control G magnitude: lam = lam / max(1,lam.max())
    L=np.fft.rfft(lam,N)
    G=np.fft.irfft(np.conj(A_scaled)*L, N)[:n]
    G = np.clip(G, None, 100) # avoid huge
    # ensure G finite, mean
    mu=np.median(G)
    z2=z + eta*(mu-G)
    # normalize z2 (not necessary) and clip
    z2=np.where(np.isfinite(z2), z2, np.max(z)+np.log(1e-12))
    z2=z2 - np.median(z2) # keep scale? But max=1 property depends scaling; score scale invariant, z shift irrelevant. Shift by max to prevent overflow.
    a2=exp(z2)
    if a2.max()==0: return None
    # rescale to max=1 and score
    b2=conv(a2)
    maxb2=b2.max()
    if maxb2>0:
       a2_norm=a2/np.sqrt(maxb2)
       S2=a2_norm.sum()
       score=2*n/(S2*S2)
    return z2, score
```
Note z shift by max; if we shift z, a2 scale changes; but we rescale max to1, invariant. Good.

But using b unscaled and A_scaled: G scales with λ (unscaled) * a_scaled. λ ~ maxb * alpha, A_scaled max conv=1. G magnitude maybe alpha * (maxb) *? b unscaled before scaling; maxb maybe base1. Fine.

We may instead set `lam = np.maximum(0, b_scaled - (1-alpha))` with b_scaled = b/maxb, so lam∈[0,1]. Use A_scaled. Then G magnitude independent. Good:
```
b_scaled = b/maxb
target_scaled = 1-alpha
lam = np.maximum(0, b_scaled - target_scaled)
```
Then update.

def perturb_z(z, rng):
   z2=z.copy()

   # block: choose width 1..n/4, factor exp normal over block
   # occasionally reshape: z2 = z2 + lowfreq

   return z2
```

Parametric candidate generation using base:
- base.

- quadratic tilt: theta1*x+theta2*x^2.
- reflected mix: a=(1-w)*base + w*base[::-1]; w 0..1 (0.1..0.9). This may help.
- shifted mix: a=(1-w)*base + w*rolled? But nonnegative.
- power of base: z=p*log(base). p in [0.5,1.5]. But exponent changes.
- noise: z=log(base)+sigma*smooth random (e.g., cumsum normalized).

   for eta in etas:

```

Let's implement `score_fast` with normalization to sum=1 to avoid scale:
```
def score_fast(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return np.inf
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S = a.sum()
    if S <= 1e-12: return np.inf
    a = a / S
    n = a.size
    N = 1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    b = np.fft.irfft(A*A, N)[:2*n-1]
    maxb = b.max()
    if maxb <= 0: return np.inf
    return float(2.0*n*maxb)
```
Since sum=1, score=2n*maxb. This is simpler and stable. Values a normalized sum=1; maxb ~1/n. For n up to 2048, a max maybe 1, okay. If some a become 1000 after normalization? original values could be huge; but we normalize before clamp? evaluate clamps before sum; values >1000 clamped. If we normalize internal, large values won't violate. In final, we can output normalized sum maybe=1; values ≤1; sum>0.01. Good.

If internal a after exp has some zeros, sum might be small? exp of large after z normalization? We'll normalize z by max before exp, so max a=1, sum ≥1. Good.

Stochastic step in z normalized:
```
def perturb_z(z, rng, sigma):
   z2=z.copy()
   method = rng.integers(3)
   if method==0:
      n=z.size; width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//20), max(2,n//2)))
      start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
      z2[start:start+width] += rng.normal(0,sigma,width)
   elif method==1:
      z2 += rng.normal(0,sigma/3,z.size)
   else:
      # low-frequency random: cumsum
      noise = np.cumsum(rng.normal(0,sigma, z.size))
      noise -= noise.mean()
      z2 += noise
   z2 = z2 - np.max(z2)
   # floor
   z2 = np.maximum(z2, -50)
   return z2
```
After exp, normalize sum; if any zero fine.

Stochastic local search can use `score_fast`.

Initial candidates:
```
def initial_candidates(n, rng):
   c=base_sequence(n)
   c = c/c.sum()
   cands=[c]
   # exponential tilts
   x=np.arange(n)
   for theta in (-3,-2,-1,-0.5,0.5,1,2,3):
       a=np.exp(0.5*np.log(c) + theta*x/(n-1)) # start base? Actually z=theta*x + maybe log c. We can use z = log(c*(1?)).
       # use base times exp(theta x/(n-1))
       a = c * np.exp(theta * x/(n-1))
       add positive clip.
   # reflected mix
   for w in (0.1,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9): a=(1-w)c + w*c[::-1]
   # suffix/prefix boost
   for t in (0.1...): a=c*(1 + t*x/(n-1)) etc.
   # power
   for p in (0.5,0.75,1.25,1.5): a=c**p
   # random noise smooth
   for sigma in (0.1,0.2,0.5): z=log(c)+lowpass noise; a=exp normalize.
   Return list
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42,budget_s=1500):
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+budget_s-5
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best_seq=None; best_score=np.inf
    # try lengths increasing maybe
    lengths=[32,64,128,256,512,1024,2048]
    # filter by time? If budget small use fewer
    for n in lengths:
       cands=initial_candidates(n,rng)
       for a in cands:
          s=score_fast(a)
          if s<best_score:
             best_score=s; best_seq=a.copy()
       # improve best for this n if time
       # choose top few candidates for this n (maybe best initial)
       best_n_seq=... local best
       if time < deadline-50:
          improved=improve_sequence(best_n_seq, rng, min(deadline, t0 + remaining split?))
          s=score_fast(improved)
          if s<best_score: update
    # final validation of top sequences
    validate_list=[best_seq]
    # add some other good sequences? Keep a list top_seqs during search.
    for seq in top_seqs[:5]:
       if time < deadline-2:
           s_eval=evaluate_sequence(seq.tolist())
           if np.isfinite(s_eval) and s_eval<best_eval: update
    # if best_eval inf (shouldn't), return best_seq normalized list
    return [float(x) for x in best_seq_for_eval]
```

Implement `improve_sequence(a0,rng,deadline)`:
```
def improve_sequence(a0, rng, deadline):
    a0 = clean normalize sum1
    z = np.log(a0 + 1e-300); z-=max(z)
    current_z=z; best_z=z; best_score=score_fast(exp(z))
    # level alphas
    alphas=[0.2,0.4,0.7,0.1,0.3,0.6,0.9]
    etas=[0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0]
    iter_count=0
    stuck=0
    # First deterministic level batch
    max_iters = 5000 if n<256 else 2000 if n<512 else 1000 if n<1024 else 500
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time > deadline-10: break
       alpha=alphas[it%len(alphas)]
       eta=etas[it%len(etas)] # maybe
       z2,s=level_step(current_z,alpha,eta)
       if s < best_score*(1-1e-10):
           current_z=z2; best_score=s; best_z=z2.copy(); stuck=0
       else:
           # try smaller eta? stochastic
           z2=s=level_step(current_z,alpha,eta*0.5)
           if s<best_score: accept
           else:
              # stochastic
              z2=perturb_z(current_z,rng,0. rises)
              s=score_fast(exp(z2))
              if s<best_score: accept
              else:
                 # accept worse with small prob for exploration
                 if rng.random() < 0.05:
                    current_z=z2
                 stuck +=1
              if stuck > 20:
                 stuck=0
                 # bigger random restart around best
                 z2=perturb_z(best_z,rng,0.8)
                 s=score_fast(exp(z2))
                 if s<best_score+0.05: current_z=z2
       iter_count+=1
    # final stochastic local search
    stoch_start=time.monotonic()
    while time < deadline-5:
       z2=perturb_z(current_z,rng,sigma=0.05-0.2)
       s=score_fast(exp(z2))
       if s<best_score:
           best_score=s; best_z=z2.copy(); current_z=z2
       # occasional level from best
       if ... level_step(best_z, alpha, eta) improve
    return exp(best_z) normalized
```

Detailed code:

```
def clean_array(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    a=a.ravel()
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return a
```
`normalize_array`:
```
def normalize_sequence(a):
    a=clean_array(a)
    if a.size==0: return None
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12:
       return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)/a.size
    return a/s
```

`base_sequence(n)` returns normalized? We can return unnormalized then normalize in candidates. To avoid small values underflow for large n, recurrence values ~1/√n not underflow. OK.

`score_fast(a)`:
```
def score_fast(a):
    a=normalize_sequence(a)
    if a is None: return np.inf
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.inf
    N=1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    b=np.fft.irfft(A*A,N)[:2*n-1]
    maxb=float(b.max())
    if not np.isfinite(maxb) or maxb < 0: maxb=0 if ...
    if maxb <= 0: return np.inf
    return float(2.0*n*maxb)
```
Because normalized sum1. b max positive.

`sequence_from_z(z)`:
```
def sequence_from_z(z):
    z=np.asarray(z,dtype=np.float64)
    z=np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, 0.0)
    if z.size==0: return None
    z=z - np.max(z)
    z=np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0)
    a=np.exp(z)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <=1e-300:
       return np.ones(z.size)/z.size
    return a/s
```

`level_step(z,alpha,eta)`:
Use `z` log of normalized sequence? It can be any. We'll compute a_raw=sequence_from_z? That normalizes sum=1, losing scale but score invariant. For level, scale not important; use sum1 sequence. Then maxb ~1/n, lam relative. G magnitude = ∑ lam a ~ alpha/n? Lambda ~1, a~1/n, G~1/n. Update z with eta*G; eta must be large ~n to have O(1) changes. But we can normalize G by its max or mean. We should standardize G to make eta scale independent. Let's do:
- a_raw = sequence_from_z(z) (sum1)
- N, A, b_raw. maxb=max(b_raw). If maxb<=0.
- b_scaled=b_raw/maxb (max1). lam=(b_scaled - (1-alpha)).clip(0,None). If lam.sum()==0 return None.
- A_scaled = A / np.sqrt(maxb)? b_raw = conv(a). If we scale a to max=1: a1=a_raw/√maxb. A1=A/√maxb. Use A1. G=irfft(conj(A1)*L)[:n]. Here L from lam (dimensionless). G scale O(1). Good.
- `g_scale = np.max(G)`; if g_scale>0: G = G/g_scale` (max1). But G may be all small; max1. `mu=np.median(G)`, `delta=G-mu`. Eta used O(1). If G all zero no change.
- z2 = z - eta * delta (since we want reduce high G). z scale: z is log(a_raw) with a_raw sum1; okay. But shift after.
- Clip z2: `z2 = np.clip(z2, np.max(z)-20.0, np.max(z)+20.0)`? Since subtracting can make large negative; -20 keeps exp ~2e-9 enough. Use -40.
- z2 = z2 - np.max(z2)
- a2=exp(z2); normalize sum? For score, we can normalize; but if all zeros, use uniform. score=score_fast(a2).
- Return z2, score.

Note z passed has sum1 sequence (max maybe 1). z2 after subtract max has max0, exp max1. score_fast normalizes sum1; okay.

Eta range: Since delta in [-1,1], eta 0.2-2. Could use 0.2 to1.

`stochastic_step(z, rng, sigma)`:
Use z from max0. Return max0.
```
def stochastic_step(z, rng, sigma):
    z2=z.copy()
    n=z.size
    mode=int(rng.integers(0,4))
    if mode==0:
       width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//100), max(2,n//3)))
       start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
       z2[start:start+width] += rng.normal(0,sigma,min(n,width))
    elif mode==1:
       z2 += rng.normal(0, sigma*0.3, n)
    elif mode==2:
       noise=np.cumsum(rng.normal(0, sigma, n))
       noise -= noise.mean()
       noise /= (np.std(noise)+1e-12)
       z2 += sigma*noise
    else:
       # swap/mirror partial
       i=int(rng.integers(0,n-1)); j=int(rng.integers(i,n))? maybe simply add spike
       idx=int(rng.integers(0,n)); z2[idx] += rng.normal(0,sigma)
    z2 = np.where(np.isfinite(z2), z2, 0.0)
    # floor to avoid all tiny
    z2 = z2 - np.max(z2)
    z2 = np.clip(z2, -45.0, 0.0)
    return z2
```

def improve_sequence(a0, rng, deadline):
    a0=normalize_sequence(a0); if None return
    z=np.log(a0+1e-300); z=z-np.max(z)
    current_z=z.copy(); best_z=z.copy()
    a=sequence_from_z(z); best_score=score_fast(a)
    n=len(a)
    iters = 20000 if n<128 else 8000 if n<256 else 4000 if n<512 else 2000 if n<1024 else 1000
    alphas=[0.2,0.5,0.8,0.3,0.6,0.1,0.9]
    etas=[0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0,0.35,0.75,1.5]
    stuck=0
    for it in range(iters):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 10: break
       alpha=alphas[it % len(alphas)]
       eta=etas[it % len(etas)]
       z2,s=level_step(current_z,alpha,eta)
       if z2 is not None and s < best_score*(1-1e-12):
           current_z=z2; best_score=s; best_z=z2.copy(); stuck=0
       else:
           # try smaller eta
           z2,s=level_step(current_z,alpha,eta*0.5)
           if ... accept
           else:
              # stochastic
              sigma = 0.1 if n>256 else 0.15
              z2=perturb; s=score_fast(sequence_from_z(z2))
              if s < best_score: accept
              else:
                 # anneal
                 temp = max(1e-4, best_score*0.005)
                 if s < best_score + temp and rng.random() < 0.1:
                    current_z=z2
                 stuck +=1
              if stuck > 10:
                 # bigger perturb from best
                 z2=perturb(best_z, rng, 0.6)
                 s=...
                 if s < best_score + 0.02: current_z=z2
                 stuck=0
    # stochastic local search until deadline
    small = max(10, int((deadline - time.monotonic())*10)) but while time
    best_score_current=best_score
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 3:
       # alternate perturb and level

           alpha=alphas[int(rng.integers(len(alphas)))]; eta=etas[...]
           z2,s=level_step(best_z,alpha,eta) or current_z?
       else:
           sigma=0.05 if n>512 else 0.1
           z2=perturb(current_z,rng,sigma)
           s=score_fast(sequence_from_z(z2))
       if s < best_score: update; current_z=z2
       # If many no improves, restart around best with larger perturb
```

```
def initial_candidates(n, rng):
    c=base_sequence(n); c=c/c.sum()
    cands=[c]
    x=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)
    xc=x/(n-1) if n>1 else 0
    for theta in (-2.0,-1.0,-0.5,0.5,1.0,2.0):
       a=c*np.exp(theta*xc)
       add
    for w in (0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,0.9):
       a=(1-w)*c + w*c[::-1]
    for w in (0.1,0.3,0.5):
       a=c*(1+w*xc)
       a=c/(1+w*(1-xc))
       # suffix boost
       a=c*(1+w*np.clip((xc-0.5)*4,0,1))
    for p in (0.7,0.9,1.1,1.3):
       a=np.power(np.clip(c,1e-12,None), p)
    # random smooth noise
    z0=np.log(np.clip(c,1e-12,None))
    for sigma in (0.1,0.3):
       noise=np.cumsum(rng.normal(0,1,n)); noise-=noise.mean(); noise/= (np.std(noise)+1e-12)
       z=z0+sigma*noise; a=np.exp(z); add
    return cands
```

Potential bug: `1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()` if 2n-1 is exact power of two? bit_length gives k for value with bit length; e.g., 1.bit_length=1, 1<<1=2 > needed 1, okay but over. If 3.bit_length=2, 1<<2=4. Good. Simpler `N = 1; while N < needed: N <<=1` but bit_length okay: For needed m, next power of two >= m is 1 << (m-1).bit_length() if m power? Example m=8, (8-1).bit_length=4, 1<<4=16, too high. `m.bit_length()` for 8 gives4, 1<<4=16. To get >=, need `1 << (m-1).bit_length()`. Let's implement helper:
```
def next_power_of_two(m):
    m = int(max(1,m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
```
For m=1 -> (0).bit_length=0 ->1. m=8 ->7.bit_length=3 ->8. Good.

`base_sequence`:
```
def base_sequence(n):
    a=np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n==0: return a
    val=1.0; a[0]=val
    for i in range(1,n):
        val *= (2*i -1)/(2*i)
        a[i]=val
    return a
```

`normalize_sequence`: if all zero, return ones/n. But if n huge, ones/n sum1. Need score? OK.

Good.

a = a_raw/s_raw  # sum1
```
Then A = rfft(a,N), b max ~1/n. maxb positive. a1 = a / sqrt(maxb) makes max=1. A1 = A/sqrt(maxb). b_scaled=b/maxb. Good.

```python
import time
import numpy as np

def next_power_of_two(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()

def clean_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = a.ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return a

def normalize_sequence(a):
    a = clean_sequence(a)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-14:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    return a / s

def base_sequence(n):
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 0:
        return a
    v = 1.0
    a[0] = v
    for i in range(1, n):
        v *= float(2 * i - 1) / float(2 * i)
        a[i] = v
    return a

def sequence_from_z(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    z = np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, 0.0)
    if z.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    z = z - float(np.max(z))
    z = np.clip(z, -45.0, 0.0)
    a = np.exp(z)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(z.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(z.size)
    return a / s

def score_fast(a):
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    N = next_power_of_two(2 * n - 1)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    b = np.fft.irfft(A * A, N)[:2 * n - 1]
    b = np.where(np.isfinite(b), b, 0.0)
    maxb = float(np.max(b))
    if maxb <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(maxb):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * maxb)

def append_candidate(cands, a):
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return None
    cands.append(a)
    return a

def initial_candidates(n, rng):
    cands = []
    if n <= 1:
        append_candidate(cands, np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64))
        return cands

    c = base_sequence(n)
    c = c / float(c.sum())
    append_candidate(cands, c)

    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    xc = x / float(n - 1)

    tilts = [-3.0, -2.0, -1.0, -0.5, -0.2, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 3.0]
    for t in tilts:
        a = c * np.exp(t * xc)
        append_candidate(cands, a)

    mixes = [0.05, 0.15, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.85]
    for w in mixes:
        a = (1.0 - w) * c + w * c[::-1]
        append_candidate(cands, a)

    boosts = [0.2, 0.5, 0.8]
    for w in boosts:
        append_candidate(cands, c * (1.0 + w * xc))
        append_candidate(cands, c * (1.0 + w * (1.0 - xc)))
        right = np.clip((xc - 0.35) / 0.3, 0.0, 1.0)
        left = np.clip((0.65 - xc) / 0.3, 0.0, 1.0)
        append_candidate(cands, c * (1.0 + w * right))
        append_candidate(cands, c * (1.0 + w * left))

    powers = [0.5, 0.75, 1.25, 1.6]
    for p in powers:
        a = np.power(np.clip(c, 1e-12, None), p)
        append_candidate(cands, a)

    phases = [0.0, 0.5 * np.pi, np.pi, 1.5 * np.pi]
    amps = [0.1, 0.25, 0.5]
    for amp in amps:
        for ph in phases:
            a = c * (1.0 + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * xc + ph))
            append_candidate(cands, a)

    z0 = np.log(np.clip(c, 1e-12, None))
    for sigma in (0.15, 0.35, 0.7):
        noise = np.cumsum(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n))
        noise -= float(noise.mean())
        sd = float(np.std(noise))
        if sd > 1e-12:
            noise /= sd
        z = z0 + sigma * noise
        z = z - float(np.max(z))
        z = np.clip(z, -40.0, 0.0)
        append_candidate(cands, np.exp(z))

    for _ in range(4):
        z = z0.copy()
        width = int(rng.integers(max(1, n // 10), max(2, n // 2)))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width)))
        stop = min(n, start + width)
        z[start:stop] += rng.normal(0.0, 0.6, stop - start)
        z = z - float(np.max(z))
        z = np.clip(z, -40.0, 0.0)
        append_candidate(cands, np.exp(z))

    return cands

def level_step(z, alpha, eta):
    if z is None:
        return None, float("inf")
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    n = z.size
    if n == 0:
        return None, float("inf")

    a0 = sequence_from_z(z)
    N = next_power_of_two(3 * n - 2)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a0, N)
    b = np.fft.irfft(A * A, N)[:2 * n - 1]
    b = np.where(np.isfinite(b), b, 0.0)
    maxb = float(np.max(b))
    if maxb <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(maxb):
        return None, float("inf")

    a1 = a0 / np.sqrt(maxb)
    A1 = A / np.sqrt(maxb)
    b1 = b / maxb

    target = 1.0 - float(alpha)
    lam = b1 - target
    lam = np.where(lam > 0.0, lam, 0.0)
    if float(np.sum(lam)) <= 0.0:
        return None, float("inf")

    L = np.fft.rfft(lam, N)
    G = np.fft.irfft(np.conj(A1) * L, N)[:n]
    G = np.where(np.isfinite(G), G, 0.0)
    G = np.where(G > 0.0, G, 0.0)
    gmax = float(np.max(G))
    if gmax <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(gmax):
        return None, float("inf")

    G = G / gmax
    med = float(np.median(G))
    delta = G - med
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(delta)):
        return None, float("inf")

    z2 = z - float(eta) * delta
    z2 = np.where(np.isfinite(z2), z2, 0.0)
    z2 = z2 - float(np.max(z2))
    z2 = np.clip(z2, -45.0, 0.0)

    a2 = sequence_from_z(z2)
    s2 = score_fast(a2)
    if not np.isfinite(s2):
        return None, float("inf")
    return z2, s2

def stochastic_step(z, rng, sigma):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    n = z.size
    if n == 0:
        return z
    z2 = z.copy()
    mode = int(rng.integers(0, 4))

    if mode == 0:
        lo = max(1, min(n, max(1, n // 80)))
        hi = max(lo, min(n, max(lo + 1, n // 4)))
        width = int(rng.integers(lo, hi + 1))
        width = min(width, n)
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        z2[start:start + width] += rng.normal(0.0, sigma, width)
    elif mode == 1:
        z2 += rng.normal(0.0, 0.3 * sigma, n)
    elif mode == 2:
        noise = np.cumsum(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
        noise -= float(noise.mean())
        sd = float(np.std(noise))
        if sd > 1e-12:
            noise /= sd
        z2 += sigma * noise
    else:
        idx = int(rng.integers(0, n))
        z2[idx] += rng.normal(0.0, sigma)

    z2 = np.where(np.isfinite(z2), z2, 0.0)
    z2 = z2 - float(np.max(z2))
    z2 = np.clip(z2, -45.0, 0.0)
    return z2

def improve_sequence(a0, rng, deadline):
    a0 = normalize_sequence(a0)
    if a0.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)

    z = np.log(np.clip(a0, 1e-300, None))
    z = z - float(np.max(z))

    best_z = z.copy()
    current_z = z.copy()
    best_score = score_fast(sequence_from_z(z))
    n = int(a0.size)

    if n <= 64:
        level_iters = 3200
        stoch_iters = 6000
    elif n <= 128:
        level_iters = 2200
        stoch_iters = 4200
    elif n <= 256:
        level_iters = 1400
        stoch_iters = 3000
    elif n <= 512:
        level_iters = 900
        stoch_iters = 2200
    elif n <= 1024:
        level_iters = 550
        stoch_iters = 1600
    else:
        level_iters = 350
        stoch_iters = 1100

    alphas = [0.15, 0.35, 0.6, 0.25, 0.75, 0.1, 0.9]
    etas = [0.25, 0.6, 1.1, 0.35, 0.9, 1.6, 0.5]
    no_improve = 0

    total_iters = level_iters + stoch_iters
    for it in range(total_iters):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 3.0:
            break

        if it < level_iters:
            alpha = alphas[it % len(alphas)]
            eta = etas[it % len(etas)]
            z2, s2 = level_step(current_z, alpha, eta)
            if z2 is not None and s2 < best_score * (1.0 - 1e-12):
                current_z = z2
                best_score = s2
                best_z = z2.copy()
                no_improve = 0
            else:
                z2b, s2b = level_step(current_z, alpha, 0.5 * eta)
                if z2b is not None and s2b < best_score * (1.0 - 1e-12):
                    current_z = z2b
                    best_score = s2b
                    best_z = z2b.copy()
                    no_improve = 0
                else:
                    if no_improve % 3 == 0:
                        sigma = 0.08 if n > 256 else 0.14
                        zr = stochastic_step(current_z, rng, sigma)
                        sr = score_fast(sequence_from_z(zr))
                        if np.isfinite(sr) and sr < best_score:
                            current_z = zr
                            best_score = sr
                            best_z = zr.copy()
                            no_improve = 0
                        else:
                            if sr < best_score + best_score * 0.003 and rng.random() < 0.12:
                                current_z = zr
                            no_improve += 1
                    else:
                        no_improve += 1

        else:
            if it % 6 == 0:
                alpha = alphas[int(rng.integers(0, len(alphas)))]
                eta = etas[int(rng.integers(0, len(etas)))]
                z2, s2 = level_step(best_z, alpha, eta)
                if z2 is not None and s2 < best_score * (1.0 - 1e-12):
                    current_z = z2
                    best_score = s2
                    best_z = z2.copy()
                    no_improve = 0
                else:
                    no_improve += 1
            else:
                sigma = 0.04 if n > 512 else (0.07 if n > 128 else 0.11)
                zr = stochastic_step(current_z, rng, sigma)
                sr = score_fast(sequence_from_z(zr))
                if np.isfinite(sr) and sr < best_score:
                    current_z = zr
                    best_score = sr
                    best_z = zr.copy()
                    no_improve = 0
                else:
                    no_improve += 1

        if no_improve > 120:
            current_z = best_z.copy()
            zbig = stochastic_step(best_z, rng, 0.55)
            sbig = score_fast(sequence_from_z(zbig))
            if np.isfinite(sbig) and sbig < best_score + 0.015:
                current_z = zbig
            no_improve = 0

    return sequence_from_z(best_z)

def add_top(top, counter, score, seq, max_items=10):
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return counter
    top.append((float(score), int(counter), np.array(seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()))
    counter += 1
    top.sort()
    if len(top) > max_items:
        top.pop()
    return counter

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1500, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_s = 1500.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget_s) or budget_s < 5.0:
        budget_s = 5.0

    deadline = t0 + max(5.0, budget_s) - 4.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")
    top = []
    counter = 0

    scale = max(0.05, min(1.0, float(budget_s) / 1500.0))
    plan = [
        (32, 8.0),
        (64, 12.0),
        (128, 22.0),
        (256, 38.0),
        (512, 64.0),
        (1024, 100.0),
        (2048, 150.0),
    ]

    for n, alloc0 in plan:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 25.0:
            break
        now = time.monotonic()
        alloc = alloc0 * scale
        sub_deadline = min(deadline - 3.0, now + max(3.0, alloc))

        cands = initial_candidates(n, rng)
        local_best = None
        local_score = float("inf")

        for a in cands:
            s = score_fast(a)
            if np.isfinite(s):
                if s < local_score:
                    local_score = s
                    local_best = a.copy()
                if s < best_score:
                    best_score = s
                    best_seq = a.copy()
                counter = add_top(top, counter, s, a, 10)
                if best_score < 1.55:
                    print(f"length {n}: fast score {best_score:.6f}")

        if local_best is not None and time.monotonic() < sub_deadline - 5.0:
            improved = improve_sequence(local_best, rng, sub_deadline)
            s = score_fast(improved)
            if np.isfinite(s):
                if s < local_score:
                    local_best = improved.copy()
                    local_score = s
                if s < best_score:
                    best_score = s
                    best_seq = improved.copy()
                counter = add_top(top, counter, s, improved, 10)
                print(f"length {n} improved: fast score {best_score:.6f}")

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0 and best_seq is not None:
        top.sort()
        if len(top) > 0 and np.isfinite(top[0][0]):
            lead = top[0][2]
        else:
            lead = best_seq
        polished = improve_sequence(lead, rng, deadline - 8.0)
        s = score_fast(polished)
        if np.isfinite(s):
            if s < best_score:
                best_score = s
                best_seq = polished.copy()
            counter = add_top(top, counter, s, polished, 10)
            print(f"final polish: fast score {best_score:.6f}")

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = normalize_sequence(base_sequence(128))
        best_score = score_fast(best_seq)

    evaluated_score = float("inf")
    evaluated_seq = None
    exact_calls = 0

    top.sort()
    for _, _, seq in top:
        if exact_calls >= 20:
            break
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0:
            break
        try:
            val = evaluate_sequence([float(v) for v in np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)])
            val = float(val)
        except Exception:
            val = float("inf")
        exact_calls += 1
        if np.isfinite(val) and val < evaluated_score:
            evaluated_score = val
            evaluated_seq = np.array(seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()

    if evaluated_seq is None:
        try:
            val = evaluate_sequence([float(v) for v in best_seq])
            val = float(val)
        except Exception:
            val = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(val):
            evaluated_score = val
            evaluated_seq = best_seq.copy()
        else:
            evaluated_seq = best_seq

    if evaluated_seq is None:
        evaluated_seq = best_seq

    final_seq = normalize_sequence(evaluated_seq)
    if final_seq.size == 0:
        final_seq = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)

    print(f"selected fast score: {best_score:.6f}, exact best seen: {evaluated_score:.6f}")
    return [float(x) for x in final_seq]
```
